# Hogwarts description catalogue

715 reviewed description records. Reading ledger: 19,036/38,426 paragraphs.

All pages below are **project reference pages (ref250-v1, 250 words)**, not print pages. Book titles and exact source paths are in the machine-readable catalogue. Rebuild the local reader to open source-text links.

[Location index](location-index.md) · [Coverage and unread ranges](coverage.md)

**Review in progress. This catalogue is not complete.**

## Book 1: Harry Potter and the Philosopher's Stone

<a id="b01-001"></a>
### B01-001 · Hogwarts — historical security

Hagrid reports that Hogwarts was among the few safe places during Voldemort’s earlier rise; Voldemort did not attempt to take the school then, reportedly because he feared Dumbledore.

- Category: history; evidence: reported; state: First rise of Voldemort, before October 1981; reported July 1991.
- Model: A historical security claim, not evidence of a particular wall, ward, or physical defensive mechanism.
- [Book 1, ch. 4, “The Keeper of the Keys”, ref p. 57, ¶107 (B01-C004-P0107)](../build/reader/book-01.html#B01-C004-P0107)

<a id="b01-002"></a>
### B01-002 · Hagrid’s hut

The boy later identified as Malfoy says Hagrid lives in a hut in the school grounds; he also alleges that Hagrid sometimes sets his bed alight while drunk and attempting magic.

- Category: grounds; evidence: reported; state: Reported July 1991; alleged recurring events undated.
- Model: Hostile second-hand report. Hut residence and a bed are provisional until observed; alleged bed fires are not an established damage state.
- [Book 1, ch. 5, “Diagon Alley”, ref p. 80–81, ¶204 (B01-C005-P0204)](../build/reader/book-01.html#B01-C005-P0204)

<a id="b01-003"></a>
### B01-003 · Hogwarts Express — London connection

The school train leaves King’s Cross platform nine and three-quarters at eleven. Passing straight through the barrier between platforms nine and ten reaches the crowded departure platform, with a scarlet steam engine and a wrought-iron arch bearing its platform name.

- Category: route; evidence: explicit; state: 1 September 1991 arrival.
- Model: Off-site transport context only; no metric distance to Hogwarts established.
- [Book 1, ch. 6, “The Journey from Platform Nine and Three-Quarters”, ref p. 92, ¶16 (B01-C006-P0016)](../build/reader/book-01.html#B01-C006-P0016)
- [Book 1, ch. 6, “The Journey from Platform Nine and Three-Quarters”, ref p. 95–96, ¶52 (B01-C006-P0052)](../build/reader/book-01.html#B01-C006-P0052)

### B01-004 · Hogwarts Express — approach landscape

After London and agricultural fields, the train passes woods, winding rivers and dark green hills; close to arrival, mountains and forests are visible in the evening darkness.

- Model: Journey sequence; the book does not identify these individual landforms or give bearings.

<a id="b01-005"></a>
### B01-005 · Arrival station to lake

The train stops at a tiny dark platform. First-years follow Hagrid’s lamp down an apparently steep, narrow path, round a bend to the lakeside. Harry thinks thick trees flank the dark path.

- Category: route; evidence: explicit; state: 1 September 1991 arrival.
- Model: Descent and bend constrain the approach. Flanking trees are Harry’s visual supposition, not confirmed vegetation geometry.
- [Book 1, ch. 6, “The Journey from Platform Nine and Three-Quarters”, ref p. 114, ¶272 (B01-C006-P0272)](../build/reader/book-01.html#B01-C006-P0272)

<a id="b01-006"></a>
### B01-006 · Hogwarts castle seen across the lake

Across a large black lake, a vast many-turreted, many-towered castle stands atop a high mountain, with sparkling windows. Approaching boats look upward at the castle and the cliff supporting it.

- Category: exterior; evidence: explicit; state: 1 September 1991 arrival.
- Model: Preserve the high relief and cross-lake vista. No tower count, compass direction, cliff height or lake width supplied.
- [Book 1, ch. 6, “The Journey from Platform Nine and Three-Quarters”, ref p. 114–115, ¶278 (B01-C006-P0278)](../build/reader/book-01.html#B01-C006-P0278)

<a id="b01-007"></a>
### B01-007 · First-year lake crossing

A fleet of small boats waits at the shore. Hagrid limits passengers to four per boat and occupies one himself. On his command all boats move together, gliding over the smooth lake towards the castle cliff.

- Category: magic; evidence: explicit; state: 1 September 1991 arrival.
- Model: A coordinated self-moving arrival transport state; four is the stated occupancy limit, not a boat dimension.
- [Book 1, ch. 6, “The Journey from Platform Nine and Three-Quarters”, ref p. 115, ¶279 (B01-C006-P0279)](../build/reader/book-01.html#B01-C006-P0279)
- [Book 1, ch. 6, “The Journey from Platform Nine and Three-Quarters”, ref p. 115, ¶280 (B01-C006-P0280)](../build/reader/book-01.html#B01-C006-P0280)

<a id="b01-008"></a>
### B01-008 · Cliff tunnel and underground harbour

At the cliff the riders duck beneath a curtain of ivy hiding a wide opening. Boats continue through a dark tunnel that seems to run directly under the castle, ending at an underground harbour where passengers step out onto rocks and pebbles.

- Category: route; evidence: explicit; state: 1 September 1991 arrival.
- Model: The apparently under-castle alignment is qualified by the narration. Opening clearance requires ducking; do not invent a vaulted dock or harbour dimensions.
- [Book 1, ch. 6, “The Journey from Platform Nine and Three-Quarters”, ref p. 115, ¶282 (B01-C006-P0282)](../build/reader/book-01.html#B01-C006-P0282)
- [Book 1, ch. 6, “The Journey from Platform Nine and Three-Quarters”, ref p. 115, ¶283 (B01-C006-P0283)](../build/reader/book-01.html#B01-C006-P0283)

### B01-009 · Underground harbour to front entrance

A passage climbs through rock from the harbour to smooth damp grass in the castle’s shadow. A flight of stone steps leads from there to a huge oak front door.

- Category: route; evidence: explicit; state: 1 September 1991 arrival.
- Model: Record rock ascent, grassy exit, steps and door in sequence; their exact arrangement and the passage’s endpoint elevation are unspecified.

### B01-010 · Entrance Hall

The enormous hall has stone walls lit by flaming torches, a flagged stone floor and a ceiling too high to distinguish. A magnificent marble staircase faces the arriving first-years and leads to upper floors.

- Category: interior; evidence: explicit; state: 1 September 1991, start-of-term feast.
- Model: Scale is compared to the whole Dursley house; no numeric height or floor plan is established.

### B01-011 · Entrance Hall — antechamber and Great Hall connection

A doorway to the arriving pupils’ right carries the noise of hundreds of voices. McGonagall first takes them to a small empty chamber off the hall; they subsequently cross the hall and enter the Great Hall through double doors.

- Category: route; evidence: explicit; state: 1 September 1991, start-of-term feast.
- Model: Right is relative to this arrival viewpoint. The small chamber is separate from the Great Hall; its side of the hall is unspecified.

### B01-012 · Antechamber walls — ghost permeability

About twenty translucent pearly-white ghosts enter through the chamber’s back wall, cross the room, and depart through the opposite wall.

- Model: Ghost paths do not require physical openings; back/opposite are local scene relations.

Thousands of candles float above four long pupil tables laid with golden plates and goblets. Another long table for teachers stands at the top of the Hall; the sorting line faces the pupils with teachers behind it.

The ceiling appears velvety black and star-spangled, so convincingly that Harry finds it difficult to believe the Hall is roofed.

The route leaves the Great Hall, climbs the marble staircase, follows portrait-lined corridors, twice passes through doorways concealed behind sliding panels and hanging tapestries, and climbs additional staircases. The portraits whisper and point; suits of armour stand along the subsequent route.

- Category: route; evidence: explicit; state: 1 September 1991, start-of-term feast.
- Model: Sequence is explicit; exact turns, floor count and allocation of the two hidden doorways to panel/tapestry mechanisms remain open.

### B01-025 · Castle staircases

Hogwarts has 142 staircases, including wide sweeping flights and narrow rickety ones. Some lead to different destinations on Fridays; some have a step halfway up that vanishes and must be jumped.

- Category: magic; evidence: explicit; state: September 1991, first school week.
- Model: A total count and examples of behaviours, not a claim that every staircase moves or that all have the same trick.

Some doors require a polite request or precise tickling to open; other apparent doors are solid walls. Portrait occupants visit one another. The layout seems to Harry to move around, and he believes suits of armour can walk.

- Category: magic; evidence: explicit; state: September 1991, first school week.
- Model: Separate narrated door/portrait behaviours from Harry’s impressions about general movement and walking armour.

<a id="b01-125"></a>
### B01-125 · Trapdoor shaft — long drop to plant

Harry lowers himself by his fingertips and drops through cold damp air onto a soft plant. From below the open trapdoor appears postage-stamp-sized; Ron and Hermione land beside him.

- Category: route; evidence: explicit; state: June 1992, approach to the Stone.
- Model: A substantial vertical shaft is supported. Hermione’s claim of being miles underground is an impression and must not set a metric depth.

<a id="b01-126"></a>
### B01-126 · Devil’s Snare chamber

The landing plant entwines pupils in long snake-like creepers, tightening as they struggle. Hermione escapes to a damp wall, recalls its preference for dark damp conditions, and blue magical flames cause it to recoil from light and warmth and release the boys.

- Category: magic; evidence: explicit; state: June 1992, approach to the Stone.
- Model: Observed plant defence with reported botanical explanation; no invented bed depth or room diameter.

The sole onward route is a stone passage sloping downward, with trickling water on its walls and audible dripping. Rustling, clinking, wings and light precede its opening into the next chamber.

<a id="b01-131"></a>
### B01-131 · Giant chess defence and damage

The pieces animate, accept three human substitutes for a black knight, bishop and rook, and move on command. Captured pieces are struck and dragged to the edge; limp black pieces accumulate along the wall. After checkmate the king discards his crown and the pieces part and bow to clear the onward door.

- Category: magic; evidence: explicit; state: June 1992, approach to the Stone.
- Model: Models a dangerous interactive chess state and displaced pieces, without assuming permanent room damage.

<a id="b01-134"></a>
### B01-134 · Potion chamber — bottle selection and passage

The riddle specifies three poisons, two nettle wines and two passage-enabling drinks; wine has poison on its left side as worded, end bottles differ, neither end leads onward, smallest/largest are safe, and second from each end taste alike. Hermione identifies the tiny smallest bottle for black fire and a rounded bottle at the right end for purple fire. The cold-tasting drinks allow her to retreat and Harry to enter the last chamber unburnt.

- Category: magic; evidence: explicit; state: June 1992, approach to the Stone.
- Model: Positions of smallest and largest within the line are not numbered in the prose; do not invent them. The smallest bottle has only one swallow left. The riddle’s left-side statement should be preserved as a puzzle constraint, not a complete bottle map.

A boarded coach sets off apparently by itself; Harry assumes an invisible horse. Its small windows, lumpy seats, mould-and-straw smell and bumping procession are described.

- Category: magic; evidence: explicit; state: 1 September 1993.
- Model: Observation and Harry’s assumption kept separate; no unseen animal species supplied from later knowledge.

<a id="b03-008"></a>
### B03-008 · Grounds gates and castle drive

Magnificent wrought-iron gates stand between stone columns topped by winged boars, with a Dementor guarding each side. Beyond them a long sloping drive rises to the many-towered castle, ending at its stone entrance steps.

- Category: exterior; evidence: explicit; state: 1 September 1993.
- Model: Gateposts and two visible sentries explicit; no total perimeter length or gate compass bearing.

<a id="b03-009"></a>
### B03-009 · Entrance Hall — right-hand Great Hall door

The giant oak doors open into a cavernous torchlit hall with a marble stair to upper floors. The Great Hall door is open to the right, showing a black cloudy enchanted ceiling.

- Category: interior; evidence: explicit; state: 1 September 1993.
- Model: Right relative to entering from the front; cloud state differs from previous arrival nights.

<a id="b03-010"></a>
### B03-010 · Entrance Hall to McGonagall’s office

McGonagall takes Harry and Hermione across the hall, up the marble staircase and along a corridor to her small office, warmed by a large fire, with desk and visitor seats. They later return down the same marble stair to the Great Hall.

- Category: route; evidence: explicit; state: 1 September 1993.
- Model: Adds small office size and route, no fresh absolute floor number.

<a id="b03-011"></a>
### B03-011 · Great Hall — sorting stool and feast

Candles hover over the long house tables; a three-legged stool and Sorting Hat are carried out after sorting. The staff table is opposite the direction Harry takes to Gryffindor; food and drink fill golden tableware and leftovers later melt away.

- Category: temporary; evidence: explicit; state: 1 September 1993.

<a id="b03-012"></a>
### B03-012 · Grounds entrances — Dementor access constraints

Dumbledore announces Dementors at every entrance, forbids leaving without permission and warns that disguises, tricks and Invisibility Cloaks cannot fool them.

- Category: magic; evidence: reported; state: 1993–94 Dementor deployment.
- Model: Character statement about security capability, not proof of an unbroken physical wall or a specified number of entrances.

<a id="b03-013"></a>
### B03-013 · Great Hall to Gryffindor Tower — new password

Gryffindors ascend the marble staircase and further corridors/stairs to the Fat Lady, whose password is now Fortuna Major. Beyond the common room the sexes take separate stairs; the boys use a spiral to the familiar round room with five four-posters.

- Category: route; evidence: explicit; state: 1 September 1993.
- Model: Reaffirms retained dormitory and separate stair routes; no floor-count reconstruction from the abbreviated journey.

<a id="b03-014"></a>
### B03-014 · Great Hall to North Tower — long stair route

Divination is at the top of North Tower, a long journey estimated by Ron at ten minutes. After seven long staircases the trio reach an unfamiliar stone-walled landing with a painting; an empty passage branches right and a window gives a partial lake view. Ron identifies that way as south.

- Category: route; evidence: explicit; state: 2 September 1993.
- Model: Seven flights are not seven floors. South is Ron’s orientation claim tied to the lake sightline, not an independently surveyed bearing.

<a id="b04-141"></a>
### B04-141 · Boris the Bewildered and bathroom entrance

Boris’s statue depicts a lost-looking wizard with his gloves on the wrong hands. Harry locates the bathroom door from this landmark, speaks Pine-fresh, enters when it creaks open, and bolts it from inside.

- Category: furnishing; evidence: explicit; state: January 1995, night bathroom visit.
- Model: Directly observed landmark and password entry following Cedric’s fifth-floor directions. The internal bolt is separate from the magical access requirement.
- [Book 4, ch. 25, “The Egg and the Eye”, ref p. 482–483, ¶4 (B04-C025-P0004)](../build/reader/book-04.html#B04-C025-P0004)

<a id="b04-142"></a>
### B04-142 · Prefects’ bathroom structure

The bathroom is made of white marble and softly lit by a candle-filled chandelier. A rectangular swimming-pool-sized bath is sunk into the centre of the floor, surrounded by roughly a hundred golden taps with differently coloured jewels in their handles; it has a diving board. Long white linen curtains cover the windows, fluffy white towels are piled in a corner, and footsteps and the egg’s cry reverberate off the marble.

- Category: interior; evidence: explicit; state: January 1995, night bathroom visit.
- Model: The water is deep enough that Harry’s feet barely reach the bottom, and the pool permits swimming lengths. These are relative scale constraints, not numeric measurements.
- [Book 4, ch. 25, “The Egg and the Eye”, ref p. 483, ¶6 (B04-C025-P0006)](../build/reader/book-04.html#B04-C025-P0006)

<a id="b04-143"></a>
### B04-143 · Prefects’ bathroom mermaid painting

The bathroom’s only painting has a golden frame and depicts a blonde mermaid sleeping on a rock, her hair moving as she snores. Myrtle describes her waking behaviour as giggling and displaying her fins.

- Category: furnishing; evidence: explicit; state: January 1995, night bathroom visit.
- Model: The sleeping image is observed; the prior animated behaviour is Myrtle’s report. Do not confuse the painted mermaid with the actual lake inhabitants.

### B04-144 · Prefects’ bathroom tap effects

Different taps supply hot water with football-sized pink and blue bubbles, thick ice-white foam, perfumed purple clouds, or arcing bouncing jets. The deep pool fills quickly; foam obscures the bather and coloured steam hangs above it.

### B04-148 · Trick stair entrapment

Halfway down the narrow shortcut Harry’s leg sinks through the trick step Neville forgets to avoid and is caught above the knee. Further struggling sinks it deeper; the map rests six steps below him, out of reach. Moody frees him by pulling his arms, after which he climbs onto the step above.

- Category: magic; evidence: explicit; state: January 1995, night bathroom visit.
- Model: Text identifies the trap with Neville’s known step. Model entrapment and assisted extraction, with traversable steps above and below, not a simple bottomless opening.

A gold-draped judges’ table stands at the muddy water’s edge.

Beyond the visible shallows Harry dives towards the middle into a dark grey-lit, foggy landscape. He passes small silver fish, blackened logs and dense weed clumps.

Harry cannot form audible spoken words underwater, producing bubbles instead. His Relashio spell produces hot water jets rather than sparks. Myrtle’s ghostly speech and the merpeople’s voices remain audible; above water the merpeople’s songs sound like screeches.

<a id="b04-218"></a>
### B04-218 · Maze Cup approach and spider damage

At a later fork Harry follows the wand-guided right-hand path and sees the gleaming Cup on a plinth a hundred yards ahead. Cedric joins from another route. A gigantic spider visible over the left hedge advances along an intersecting path; it lifts and drops Harry twelve feet, then combined Stunning Spells topple it sideways, flattening a hedge and filling the path with tangled legs. The Cup is only feet from Cedric after the fight.

- Category: temporary; evidence: explicit; state: June 1995 preparation and 24 June third task; history where stated.
- Model: Preserve local crossing, final straight view, plinth and temporary hedge damage. The twelve-foot fall is not a measured spider height.

After the Cup transports them away, Harry notes that even the mountains surrounding Hogwarts are no longer present.

Crouch reports bringing the captured real Moody, clothes and detectors to Hogwarts in Moody’s own magical trunk, keeping him alive under Imperius for hair and interrogation, and stealing Polyjuice ingredients from Snape’s dungeon stores. <a id="b04-227"></a>
### B04-227 · Moody’s seven-lock trunk

Each keyhole opens a different interior of the same trunk: first spellbooks, then broken Sneakoscopes, parchment, quills and a silvery Invisibility Cloak, with other unspecified contents in compartments three to six. The seventh reveals a pit-like room ten feet below containing the real Moody. Dumbledore climbs down, covers him against the cold and climbs back out.

- Category: magic; evidence: explicit; state: 24 June 1995 return and interrogation; prior-year events where reported.
- Model: Nested magical interior, not proof of a cellar excavated beneath the office. No ladder is described; the ten-foot drop is explicit.
